# New Mexico

**New Mexico**[^cite_note-6] is a landlocked state in the [Southwestern](179571.md) region of the United States. It is one of the [Mountain States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_States) of the southern [Rocky Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Mountains), sharing the [Four Corners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_Corners) region with [Utah](31716.md), [Colorado](5399.md), and [Arizona](21883824.md). It also borders the state of [Texas](29810.md) to the east and southeast, [Oklahoma](22489.md) to the northeast, and shares [an international border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico-United_States_border) with the [Mexican states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_states) of [Chihuahua](23962301.md) and [Sonora](83750.md) to the south. New Mexico's largest city is [Albuquerque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albuquerque), and its [state capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States) is [Santa Fe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe,_New_Mexico), the oldest state capital in the U.S.—founded in 1610 as the government seat of [Nuevo México](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe_de_Nuevo_M%C3%A9xico) in [New Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Spain)—and the highest in elevation, at 7,000 feet (2,134 m).

New Mexico is the [fifth-largest of the fifty states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_area) by area, but with just over 2.1 million residents, ranks [36th in population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_population) and [45th in population density](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_population_density). Its climate and geography are highly varied, ranging from forested mountains to sparse deserts; the [northern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_New_Mexico) and [eastern regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_New_Mexico) exhibit a colder [alpine climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpine_climate), while the west and south are warmer and [more arid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desert_climate). The [Rio Grande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande) and [its fertile valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande_Valley_\(New_Mexico\)) runs north-to-south, creating a [riparian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riparian_zone) biome through the [center of the state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_New_Mexico) that supports a [bosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosque) habitat and distinct [Albuquerque Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albuquerque_Basin) climate. One-third of New Mexico's land is federally owned, including many protected [wilderness areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilderness_areas_of_the_United_States), 15 [national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_areas_in_the_United_States_National_Park_System) and monuments, and [three UNESCO World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_the_United_States) wholly contained within the state—the most of any U.S. state.

Since [time immemorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prehistory), New Mexico was home to [Pueblo peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo_peoples), the [Mogollon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mogollon_culture) culture, and [Ute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ute_Mountain_Ute_Tribe). The Pueblo peoples occupied several dozen villages, mostly in northern New Mexico's Rio Grande valley. The [Navajo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navajo_Nation) and [Apache](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apache) arrived in the late 1400s, the [Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Empire) in 1598, and the [Comanche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comanche) in the early 1700s. The Spanish colony of [Nuevo México](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe_de_Nuevo_M%C3%A9xico) became a [peripheral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Periphery_countries) part of the [viceroyalty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viceroy) of [New Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Spain). The 1680 [Pueblo Revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo_Revolt) was the first successful revolution against colonial rule in the Americas. In 1692 Spanish colonial rule returned, but the colony fell under the influence of [Comancheria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comancheria) with the [Comanche-Spanish peace treaties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comanche-Spanish_peace_treaties) of 1785 and 1786, isolated by its rugged terrain and distance from [Mexico City](18987.md). Upon [Mexican independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_War_of_Independence) in 1821, New Mexico became an [autonomous territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_administrative_division) that saw an [1837 Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Arriba_Rebellion) against the central government, with increasing economic ties to the United States. After the [Mexican–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican%E2%80%93American_War) in 1848, the U.S. [annexed New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Cession) as part of the larger [New Mexico Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Mexico_Territory), which was central to [U.S. westward expansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_frontier). New Mexico was a territory for 64 years before it was [admitted to the Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admission_to_the_Union) as the 47th state on January 6, 1912. During [World War II](32927.md), New Mexico hosted several [key facilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Y) of the [Manhattan Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Project), which developed the world's first [atomic bomb](21785.md), and was the site of the first nuclear test, [Trinity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinity_\(nuclear_test\)) in 1945.

The convergence of Pueblo, Apache, Navajo, Comanche, Ute, Spanish, Mexican, and [American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_frontier) influences throughout history led to a [culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_New_Mexico) with unique [cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Mexican_cuisine), [Spanish dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Mexican_Spanish), [music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Mexico_music), and the [Pueblo Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo_Revival_architecture) and [Territorial styles of architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_Style). As one of nine [majority-minority states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majority_minority_in_the_United_States), it has the nation's highest proportion of [Hispanic and Latino Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic_and_Latino_Americans) and the second-highest proportion of Native Americans, after [Alaska](624.md). It is home to one-third of the [Navajo Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navajo_Nation), 19 [federally recognized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_federally_recognized_tribes_in_the_United_States) [Pueblo communities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo), and three federally recognized [Apache](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apache) tribes. The large Latino population includes [Hispanos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanos_of_New_Mexico) descended from families in the colonies period alongside more recent [Mexican Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Americans). The [New Mexican flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_New_Mexico), which is among the most recognizable in the U.S., reflects [indigenous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zia_people_\(New_Mexico\)) and [Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Spain) inspiration.

[New Mexico's economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_New_Mexico) is highly diversified; major sectors include mining, oil and gas, aerospace, [media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Albuquerque,_New_Mexico), and [film.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_films_shot_in_New_Mexico) Its total [real gross domestic product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_gross_domestic_product) (GDP) was \$152.778 billion in 2025, with a GDP per capita of \$61,645. New Mexico often ranks low in wealth, healthcare, and education, but has some of the highest concentrations of scientists, engineers, along with other highly skilled workers, and one of the most educated (albeit very small) counties in the U.S.: [Los Alamos County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Alamos_County,_New_Mexico). The federal government has a significant presence in New Mexico, including [U.S. military](32212.md) installations such as [White Sands Missile Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Sands_Missile_Range) and [Kirtland Air Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirtland_Air_Force_Base), and [Department of Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Energy_National_Laboratories) research centers such as the [Sandia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandia_National_Laboratories) and [Los Alamos National Laboratories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Alamos_National_Laboratory).

## Etymology

New Mexico received its name long before the present-day country of Mexico won independence from Spain and adopted that name in 1821. The name "Mexico" derives from [Nahuatl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahuatl) and originally referred to the heartland of the [Mexica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexica), the rulers of the [Aztec Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aztec_Empire), in the [Valley of Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valley_of_Mexico).[^cite_note-7][^cite_note-8] After [conquering the Aztecs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_conquest_of_the_Aztec_Empire) in the early 16th century, the Spanish began exploring what is now the Southwestern United States, calling it *Nuevo México* after the region known as the [Kingdom of Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Mexico). In 1581, the [Chamuscado and Rodríguez Expedition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamuscado_and_Rodr%C3%ADguez_Expedition) named the region north of the Rio Grande *San Felipe del Nuevo México*.[^cite_note-9] The Spaniards had hoped to find wealthy indigenous cultures similar to the Mexica; the indigenous cultures of New Mexico proved to be unrelated to the Mexica and lacking in riches, but the name persisted.[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11]

Before statehood in 1912, the name "New Mexico" loosely applied to various configurations of territories in the same general area, which [evolved throughout the Spanish, Mexican, and U.S. periods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_evolution_of_New_Mexico), but typically encompassed most of present-day New Mexico along with sections of neighboring states.[^cite_note-12]

## History

Main article: [History of New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_New_Mexico)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of New Mexico history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_New_Mexico_history).

See also: [Territorial evolution of New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_evolution_of_New_Mexico)



Ancestral Pueblo territory shown in pink over New Mexico

### Prehistory

The first known inhabitants of New Mexico were members of the [Clovis culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clovis_culture) of [Paleo-Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleo-Indians).[^cite_note-Murphy_2000-13]<sup>: 19 </sup> Footprints discovered in 2017 suggest that humans may have been present in the region as long ago as 21,000–23,000 BC.[^cite_note-14] Later inhabitants include the [Mogollon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mogollon_culture), [Ancestral Pueblo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancestral_Puebloans) cultures, which are characterized by sophisticated pottery work and urban development;[^cite_note-Simmons_1988-15]<sup>: 52 </sup> [pueblos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo) or their remnants, like those at [Acoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acoma_Pueblo), [Taos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taos_Pueblo), and [Chaco Culture National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaco_Culture_National_Historical_Park), indicate the scale of [Ancestral Puebloan dwellings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancestral_Puebloan_dwellings) within the area. These cultures and the [Ute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ute_people) form part of the broader [Oasisamerica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oasisamerica) region of pre-Contact North America.[^cite_note-16]

Navajos may be descendants of the lost Naha tribe, a [Slavey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavey_people) tribe from the [Nahanni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahanni_National_Park_Reserve) region west of Great Slave Lake.[^cite_note-17]

The vast trade networks of the Ancestral Puebloans led to legends throughout [Mesoamerica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesoamerica) and the [Aztec Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aztec_Empire) ([Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexica)) of an unseen northern empire that rivaled their own, which they called *Yancuic Mexico*, literally translated as "a new Mexico".

### First Spanish colonial period

Main articles: [Seven Golden Cities of Cibola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Golden_Cities_of_Cibola) and [Santa Fe de Nuevo México](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe_de_Nuevo_M%C3%A9xico)

See also: [Spanish peace treaties with the Comanche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_peace_treaties_with_the_Comanche)

Aztec legends of a prosperous empire to their north became the primary basis for the mythical [Seven Cities of Gold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Cities_of_Gold), which spurred exploration by Spanish [conquistadors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conquistador) following their [conquest of the Aztecs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_conquest_of_the_Aztec_Empire) in the early 16th century; prominent explorers included [Álvar Núñez Cabeza de Vaca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81lvar_N%C3%BA%C3%B1ez_Cabeza_de_Vaca), [Andrés Dorantes de Carranza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9s_Dorantes_de_Carranza), [Alonso del Castillo Maldonado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alonso_del_Castillo_Maldonado), [Estevanico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estevanico), and [Marcos de Niza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcos_de_Niza).[^cite_note-Levin_Rojo_2002-18]

In 1539, [Esteban de Dorantes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esteban_de_Dorantes) became the first person from the [Old World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_World) to reach New Mexico, where he was killed by Pueblo peoples.[^cite_note-19][^cite_note-20] [Francisco Vázquez de Coronado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_V%C3%A1zquez_de_Coronado) arrived in 1540. In 1598 [conquistador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conquistador) [Juan de Oñate y Salazar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_de_O%C3%B1ate_y_Salazar) began the Spanish colonization of New Mexico.[^cite_note-Hendricks_2015_pp._427–428-21] The campaign included Oñate's 1599 [Ácoma Massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81coma_Massacre).[^cite_note-intermountainhistories.org-22] Oñate arrived at Acoma Pueblo on January 21 with an army including cannons and muskets. The Spanish set fire to the Pueblo.[^cite_note-intermountainhistories.org-22] Approximately 800–1000 Ácoma were murdered.[^cite_note-ReferenceA-23] After this initial attack, on February 12, Oñate ordered that the right foot be cut off every man over 25.[^cite_note-intermountainhistories.org-22]

The settlement of *[La Villa Real de la Santa Fe de San Francisco de Asís](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe,_New_Mexico)*—modern day Santa Fe – was established by [Pedro de Peralta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_de_Peralta) as a more permanent capital at the foot of the [Sangre de Cristo Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangre_de_Cristo_Mountains) in 1610.[^cite_note-Simmons_Last_Conquistador-24]<sup>: 182 </sup>[^cite_note-Levin_Rojo_2002-18]

### Pueblo Revolt

Main articles: [Pueblo Revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo_Revolt) and [Pueblo confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo_confederation?action=edit\&redlink=1)

The first successful revolution against European rule in the Americas occurred with the 1680 [Pueblo Revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo_Revolt). From [Taos Pueblo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taos_Pueblo), the Pueblo leader [Po'pay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Po'pay) rallied the Pueblo peoples into expelling the Spanish from New Mexico. He would rule the Pueblo confederation until his death.[^cite_note-25][^cite_note-archaeology.org-26]

### Return of the Spanish

After the death of Pueblo leader Po'pay, some Pueblo leaders invited the Spanish to return to New Mexico. [Diego de Vargas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diego_de_Vargas) led the return of the Spanish in 1692.[^cite_note-Simmons_1988-15]<sup>: 68–75 </sup> with Puebloans offered greater cultural and religious liberties.[^cite_note-27][^cite_note-28][^cite_note-Murphy_2000-13]<sup>: 6, 48 </sup> Compared to the previous colonial regime, the Spanish sought relative compromise with the Pueblos on religious matters, seeking to avoid another revolt. Beyond forging better relations with the Pueblos, governors were forbearing in their approach to the indigenous peoples, such as was with governor [Tomás Vélez Cachupín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom%C3%A1s_V%C3%A9lez_Cachup%C3%ADn);[^cite_note-Simmons_2012-29] the comparatively large reservations in New Mexico and [Arizona](21883824.md) are partly a legacy of Spanish treaties recognizing indigenous land claims in Nuevo México.

Returning settlers founded *[La Villa de Alburquerque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albuquerque,_New_Mexico)* in 1706 at [Old Town Albuquerque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Town_Albuquerque) as a trading center for existing surrounding communities such as [Barelas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barelas), [Isleta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo_of_Isleta), [Los Ranchos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Ranchos_de_Albuquerque,_New_Mexico), and [Sandia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandia_Pueblo);[^cite_note-Simmons_1988-15]<sup>: 84 </sup> it was named for the viceroy of New Spain, [Francisco Fernández de la Cueva, 10th Duke of Alburquerque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Fern%C3%A1ndez_de_la_Cueva,_10th_Duke_of_Alburquerque).[^cite_note-30] Governor [Francisco Cuervo y Valdés](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Cuervo_y_Vald%C3%A9s) established the villa in [Tiguex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albuquerque_metropolitan_area) to provide [free trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_trade) access and facilitate cultural exchange in the region.

### Increased Comanche influence

See also: [Comanche-Spanish peace treaties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comanche-Spanish_peace_treaties)

To encourage settlement in its vulnerable periphery amidst the increased power and reach of Comanche raids, Spain awarded land grants to European settlers in Nuevo México; due to the scarcity of water throughout the region, the vast majority of colonists resided in the central valley of the Rio Grande and its tributaries. Most communities were walled enclaves consisting of adobe houses that opened onto a plaza, from which four streets ran outward to small, private agricultural plots and orchards; these were watered by *[acequias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acequia),* community owned and operated irrigation canals. Just beyond the wall was the *[ejido](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ejido)*, communal land for grazing, firewood, or recreation.

While New Mexico was legally part of New Spain, it fell under the influence of [Comancheria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comancheria) in with the 1785 and 1786 [Comanche-Spanish peace treaties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comanche-Spanish_peace_treaties) and became a *de facto* [protectorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protectorate), with New Mexico refusing multiple orders from Madrid and Mexico City to go to war with the Comanche.[^cite_note-Hamalainen-31][^cite_note-NMHR_Vol_80_Num_1_Art_4-32] Nevertheless, relations between the various indigenous groups and Spanish settlers remained nebulous and complex, varying from trade and commerce to cultural assimilation and intermarriage to total warfare. During most of the 18th century, raids by [Navajo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navajo), [Apache](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apache), and especially [Comanche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comanche) inhibited the growth and prosperity of the New Mexico. The region's harsh environment and remoteness, surrounded by hostile Native Americans, fostered a greater degree of self-reliance, as well as pragmatic cooperation, between the [Pueblo peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo_people) and colonists. Many indigenous communities enjoyed a large measure of autonomy well into the late 19th century due to the improved governance.

By 1800, the population of New Mexico had reached 25,000 (not including indigenous inhabitants), far exceeding the territories of California and Texas.[^cite_note-33]

### Mexican territorial period

See also: [Río Arriba Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C3%ADo_Arriba_Rebellion) and [Cantón de Nuevo México](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cant%C3%B3n_de_Nuevo_M%C3%A9xico)



Territory of [Santa Fe de Nuevo México](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe_de_Nuevo_M%C3%A9xico) when it belonged to Mexico in 1824

As part of New Spain, the province of New Mexico became part of the [First Mexican Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Mexican_Empire) in 1821 following the [Mexican War of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_War_of_Independence).[^cite_note-Simmons_1988-15]<sup>: 109 </sup> Upon its secession from Mexico in 1836, the [Republic of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Texas) claimed the portion east of the [Rio Grande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande), based on the erroneous assumption that the older Hispanic settlements of the upper Rio Grande were the same as the newly established Mexican settlements of Texas. The [Texan Santa Fe Expedition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texan_Santa_Fe_Expedition) was launched to seize the contested territory but failed with the capture and imprisonment of the entire army by the Hispanic New Mexico militia.

At the turn of the 19th century, France still claimed northeastern New Mexico—the area north of the Canadian River and east of the [Sangre de Cristos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangre_de_Cristo_Range)—before selling it in the [1803 Louisiana Purchase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_Purchase). In 1812, the U.S. reclassified the land as part of the [Missouri Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missouri_Territory). This region of New Mexico (along with territory comprising present-day southeastern Colorado, the [Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Panhandle) and [Oklahoma Panhandles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oklahoma_Panhandle), and southwestern [Kansas](16716.md)) was ceded to Spain under the [Adams-Onis Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adams-Onis_Treaty) in 1819.

When the [First Mexican Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Mexican_Republic) began to transition into the [Centralist Republic of Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centralist_Republic_of_Mexico), they began to centralize power ignoring the sovereignty of Santa Fe and disregarding Pueblo land rights. The end of Spanish [protectionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protectionism) led to New Mexico becoming financially and politically tied to the U.S., and preferring trade along the [Santa Fe Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe_Trail). This led to the [Río Arriba Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C3%ADo_Arriba_Rebellion) in 1837, which established the [Cantón de Nuevo México](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cant%C3%B3n_de_Nuevo_M%C3%A9xico) led by [Taos Pueblo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taos_Pueblo) members [José María González](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mar%C3%ADa_Gonz%C3%A1lez) and [Pablo Montoya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pablo_Montoya).[^cite_note-Crandall-34][^cite_note-Lecompte_1985_p.-35] The rebellion began with the assassination of Centralist governor [Albino Pérez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albino_P%C3%A9rez) and ended with the execution of González was executed by governor [Manuel Armijo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Armijo). Subsequent governor [Juan Bautista Vigil y Alarid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Bautista_Vigil_y_Alarid) agreed with some of the underlying sentiment.[^cite_note-Lecompte_1985_p.-35]

### Initial American territorial period

Main articles: [Texas annexation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_annexation), [U.S. provisional government of New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._provisional_government_of_New_Mexico), [Mexican–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican%E2%80%93American_War), [Treaty of Guadalupe Hidalgo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Guadalupe_Hidalgo), [Mexican Cession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Cession), [Organic act § List of organic acts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organic_act#List_of_organic_acts), [New Mexico Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Mexico_Territory), [Gadsden Purchase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadsden_Purchase), [Ordinance of Secession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordinance_of_Secession), [Confederate Arizona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_Arizona), [Confederate States of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_States_of_America), and [New Mexico Territory in the American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Mexico_Territory_in_the_American_Civil_War)

Following the victory of the United States in the Mexican–American War (1846–48), Mexico [ceded its northern territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Guadalupe_Hidalgo) to the U.S., including California, Texas, and New Mexico.[^cite_note-Simmons_1988-15]<sup>: 132 </sup> The Americans were initially heavy-handed in their treatment of former Mexican citizens, triggering the [Taos Revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taos_Revolt) in 1847 by Hispanos and their Pueblo allies; the insurrection led to the death of territorial governor [Charles Bent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Bent) and the collapse of the civilian government established by [Stephen W. Kearny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_W._Kearny). In response, the U.S. government appointed local [Donaciano Vigil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donaciano_Vigil) as governor to better represent New Mexico,[^cite_note-Crutchfield_1995_p.-36] and also vowed to accept the land rights of [Hispanos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanos_of_New_Mexico) and grant them citizenship. In 1864, President [Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln) symbolized the recognition of Native land rights with the Lincoln Canes, [sceptres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sceptre) [of office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staff_of_office) gifted to each of the Pueblos, a tradition dating back to Spanish and Mexican eras.[^cite_note-NCAI-37][^cite_note-Mexican_2022-38]

After the [Republic of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Texas) was admitted as a state in 1846, it attempted to claim the eastern portion of New Mexico east of the Rio Grande, while the [California Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Republic) and [State of Deseret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Deseret) each claimed parts of western New Mexico. Under the [Compromise of 1850](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compromise_of_1850), these regions were forced by the U.S. government to drop their claims, Texas received \$10 million in federal funds, California was granted statehood, and officially establishing the [Utah Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utah_Territory); therein recognizing most of New Mexico's historically established land claims.[^cite_note-Simmons_1988-15]<sup>: 135 </sup> Pursuant to the compromise, Congress established the [New Mexico Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Mexico_Territory) in September of that year;[^cite_note-39] it included most of present-day Arizona and New Mexico, along with the [Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Vegas,_Nevada) [Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Vegas_Valley) and what would later become [Clark County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clark_County,_Nevada) in [Nevada](21216.md).

In 1853 the U.S. acquired the mostly desert southwestern bootheel of the state, along with Arizona's land south of the Gila River, in the [Gadsden Purchase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadsden_Purchase), which was needed for the right-of-way to encourage construction of a [transcontinental railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transcontinental_railroad).[^cite_note-Simmons_1988-15]<sup>: 136 </sup>[^cite_note-40]

### Postwar American territorial period

The end of the Civil War saw [rapid economic development and settlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_frontier) in New Mexico, which attracted homesteaders, ranchers, cowboys, businessmen, and outlaws;[^cite_note-online.nmartmuseum.org-41] many of the [folklore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore) characters of the [Western genre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_\(genre\)) had their origins in New Mexico, most notably businesswoman [Maria Gertrudis Barceló](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Gertrudis_Barcel%C3%B3), outlaw [Billy the Kid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billy_the_Kid), and lawmen [Pat Garrett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pat_Garrett) and [Elfego Baca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elfego_Baca). The influx of "Anglo Americans" from the eastern U.S. (which include African Americans and recent European immigrants) reshaped the state's economy, culture, and politics. Into the late 19th century, the majority of New Mexicans remained ethnic mestizos of mixed Spanish and Native American ancestry (primarily Pueblo, Navajo, Apache, Genízaro, and Comanche), many of whom had roots going back to Spanish settlement in the 16th century; this distinctly New Mexican ethnic group became known as [Hispanos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanos_of_New_Mexico) and developed a more pronounced identity vis-a-vis the newer Anglo arrivals. Politically, they still controlled most town and county offices through local elections, and wealthy ranching families commanded considerable influence, preferring business, [legislative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislature), and [judicial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judiciary) relations with fellow indigenous New Mexican groups. By contrast, Anglo Americans, who were "outnumbered, but well-organized and growing"[^cite_note-Montgomery-42] tended to have more ties to the territorial government, whose officials were appointed by the U.S. federal government; subsequently, newer residents of New Mexico generally favored maintaining territorial status, which they saw as a check on Native and Hispano influence.



"The indigenous people of northern New Mexico" by [Balduin Möllhausen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balduin_M%C3%B6llhausen), 1861

A consequence of the civil war was intensifying conflict with indigenous peoples, which was part of the broader American [Indian Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Indian_Wars) along the frontier. The withdrawal of troops and material for the war effort had prompted raids by hostile tribes, and the federal government moved to subdue the many native communities that had been effectively autonomous throughout the colonial period. Following the elimination of the Confederate threat, Brigadier General [James Carleton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Henry_Carleton), who had assumed command of the Military Department of New Mexico in 1862, led what he described as a "merciless war against all hostile tribes" that aimed to "force them to their knees, and then confine them to reservations where they could be Christianized and instructed in agriculture."[^cite_note-online.nmartmuseum.org-41] With famed frontiersman [Kit Carson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kit_Carson) placed in charge of troops in the field, powerful indigenous groups such as the [Navajo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navajo_people), [Mescalero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mescalero) Apache, [Kiowa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiowa), and [Comanche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comanche) were brutally [pacified](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacification_\(military_action\)) through a scorched earth policy, and thereafter forced into barren and remote reservations. Sporadic conflicts continued into the late 1880s, most notably [the guerilla campaigns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victorio's_War) led by Apache [chiefs Victorio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victorio) and his son-in-law [Nana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nana_\(chief\)).

The political and cultural clashes between these competing ethnic groups sometimes culminated in mob violence, including lynchings of Native, Hispanic, and Mexican peoples, as was attempted at the [Frisco shootout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frisco_shootout) in 1884. Nevertheless, prominent figures from across these communities, and from both the [Democratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_of_New_Mexico) and [Republican parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Party_of_New_Mexico), attempted to fight this prejudice and forge a more cohesive, multiethnic New Mexican identity; they include lawmen [Baca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elfego_Baca) and [Garrett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pat_Garrett), and governors [Curry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Curry_\(politician\)), [Hagerman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbert_James_Hagerman), and [Otero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Antonio_Otero_\(born_1859\)).[^cite_note-Van_Holtby_2012_p.-43][^cite_note-de_Aragón_2020_p.-44] Indeed, some territorial governors, like [Lew Wallace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lew_Wallace), had served in both the Mexican and American militaries.[^cite_note-Cain_2019-45]

In the early to mid-20th century, the art presence in Santa Fe grew, and it became known as one of the world's great art centers.[^cite_note-516_ARTS-46][^cite_note-ARTnews.com_2022-47] The presence of artists such as [Georgia O'Keeffe](311615.md) attracted many others, including those along [Canyon Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canyon_Road,_Santa_Fe,_New_Mexico).[^cite_note-Vin_2021-48] In the late 20th century, Native Americans were authorized by federal law to establish gaming casinos on their reservations under certain conditions, in states which had authorized such gaming. Such facilities have helped tribes close to population centers generate revenues for reinvestment in the economic development and welfare of their peoples. The [Albuquerque metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albuquerque_metropolitan_area) is home to several casinos as a result.[^cite_note-Albuquerque_Journal_2021-49]

### Statehood

Main articles: [Admission to the Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admission_to_the_Union) and [List of U.S. states by date of admission to the Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_date_of_admission_to_the_Union)



A [Hispano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanos_of_New_Mexico) boy in [Chamisal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamisal), 1940

The [United States Congress](31756.md) admitted New Mexico as the [47th state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_date_of_admission_to_the_Union) on January 6, 1912.[^cite_note-Simmons_1988-15]<sup>: 166 </sup> It had been eligible for statehood 60 years earlier, but was delayed due to the perception that its majority [Hispanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_American) population was "alien" to U.S. culture and political values.[^cite_note-50] When the U.S. entered the First World War roughly five years later, New Mexicans volunteered in significant numbers, in part to prove their loyalty as full-fledged citizens of the U.S. The state ranked fifth in the nation for military service, enlisting more than 17,000 recruits from all 33 counties; over 500 New Mexicans were killed in the war.[^cite_note-51] Indigenous-Hispanic families had long been established since the Spanish and Mexican era,[^cite_note-Oropeza_2019_pp._162–189-52] but most American settlers in the state had an uneasy relationship with the large Native American tribes.[^cite_note-Noel_2011_pp._430–467-53] Most indigenous New Mexicans lived on [reservations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_reservation) and near old *placitas* and *villas*. In 1924, Congress passed a law granting all Native Americans U.S. citizenship and the right to vote in federal and state elections. However, Anglo-American arrivals into New Mexico enacted [Jim Crow laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Crow_laws) against Hispanos, Hispanic Americans, and those who did not pay taxes, targeting indigenous affiliated individuals;[^cite_note-suffrage-54] because Hispanics often had [interpersonal relationships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interpersonal_relationship) with indigenous peoples, they were often subject to [segregation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_segregation_in_the_United_States), [social inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_inequality_in_the_United_States), and [employment discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Employment_discrimination).[^cite_note-Noel_2011_pp._430–467-53]



A [homesteader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homesteading) and his children at the New Mexico Fair in [Pie Town, New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pie_Town,_New_Mexico), 1940

During the fight for [women's suffrage in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women's_suffrage_in_the_United_States), New Mexico's Hispano and Mexican women at the forefront included Trinidad Cabeza de Baca, Dolores "Lola" Armijo, Mrs. James Chavez, Aurora Lucero, Anita "Mrs. Secundino" Romero, Arabella "Mrs. Cleofas" Romero and her daughter, Marie.[^cite_note-Cahill_2020-55][^cite_note-Strykowski_2022-56]

A major oil discovery in 1928 near the town of [Hobbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hobbs,_New_Mexico) brought greater wealth to the state, especially in surrounding [Lea County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lea_County,_New_Mexico).[^cite_note-57] The New Mexico Bureau of Mines and Mineral Resources called it "the most important single discovery of oil in New Mexico's history".[^cite_note-AOGHS-58] Nevertheless, agriculture and cattle ranching remained the primary economic activities.

### World War II and aftermath

New Mexico was [greatly transformed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Mexico_during_World_War_II) by the U.S. entry into the [Second World War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_World_War) in December 1941. As in the First World War, [patriotism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriotism_in_the_United_States) ran high among New Mexicans, including among marginalized Hispanic and indigenous communities; on a per capita basis, New Mexico produced more volunteers, and suffered more casualties, than any other state. The war also spurred economic development, particularly in extractive industries, with the state becoming a leading supplier of several strategic resources. New Mexico's rough terrain and geographic isolation made it an attractive location for several sensitive military and scientific installations; the most famous was [Los Alamos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Alamos,_New_Mexico), one of the central facilities of the [Manhattan Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Project), where the first [atomic bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomic_bomb) were designed and manufactured. The first bomb was tested at [Trinity site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinity_site) in the desert between [Socorro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socorro,_New_Mexico) and [Alamogordo](125944.md), which is today part of the [White Sands Missile Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Sands_Missile_Range).[^cite_note-Simmons_1988-15]<sup>: 179–180 </sup>

As a legacy of the Second World War, New Mexico continues to receive large amounts of federal government spending on major military and research institutions that continue to this day.[^cite_note-59] In addition to the White Sands Missile Range, the state hosts three U.S. Air Force bases that were established or expanded during the war. While the high military presence brought considerable investment, it has also been the center of controversy; on May 22, 1957, a B-36 [accidentally dropped](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_17_nuclear_bomb#1957_incident) a nuclear bomb 4.5 miles from the control tower while landing at [Kirtland Air Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirtland_Air_Force_Base) in Albuquerque; only its conventional "trigger" detonated.[^cite_note-60][^cite_note-61] The [Los Alamos National Laboratory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Alamos_National_Laboratory) and [Sandia National Laboratories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandia_National_Laboratories), two of the nation's leading [federal scientific research facilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Energy_National_Laboratories), originated from the Manhattan Project. The focus on high technology is still a top priority of the state, to the extent that it became a center for [unidentified flying objects](31814.md), especially following the 1947 [Roswell incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roswell_incident).

New Mexico's population nearly doubled from roughly 532,000 in 1940 to over 954,000 by 1960.[^cite_note-1990_CPH2-62][^cite_note-63] In addition to federal personnel and agencies, many residents and businesses moved to the state, particularly from the northeast, often drawn by its warm climate and low taxes.[^cite_note-64] The pattern continues into the 21st century, with New Mexico adding over 400,000 residents between 2000 and 2020.

Native Americans from New Mexico fought for the United States in both world wars. Returning veterans were disappointed to find their civil rights limited by state discrimination. In Arizona and New Mexico, veterans challenged state laws or practices prohibiting them from voting. In 1948, after veteran Miguel Trujillo Sr. of [Isleta Pueblo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isleta_Pueblo) was told by the county registrar that he could not register to vote, he filed suit against the county in federal district court. A three-judge panel overturned as unconstitutional New Mexico's provisions that Native Americans who did not pay taxes (and could not document if they had paid taxes) could not vote.[^cite_note-suffrage-54][^cite_note-65]

### 21st century

In the 21st century, employment growth areas in New Mexico include [electronic circuitry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_circuit), [scientific research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_research), information technology, [casinos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_casinos_in_New_Mexico), [art of the American Southwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_of_the_American_Southwest), food, [film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_films_shot_in_New_Mexico), and [media, particularly in Albuquerque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Albuquerque,_New_Mexico).[^cite_note-66] The state was the founding location of [Micro Instrumentation and Telemetry Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Micro_Instrumentation_and_Telemetry_Systems), which led to the founding of [Microsoft](19001.md) in Albuquerque.[^cite_note-Microsoft_Learn_1975-67] [Intel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intel) maintains their F11X in [Rio Rancho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Rancho,_New_Mexico), which also hosts an IT center for [HP Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HP_Inc.)[^cite_note-Cutress_2021-68][^cite_note-Lorenz_2022-69] [New Mexico's culinary scene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Mexican_cuisine) became recognized and is now a source of revenue for the state.[^cite_note-Washington_2018-70][^cite_note-Kennedy_2022-71][^cite_note-Seymore_2019-72] [Albuquerque Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albuquerque_Studios) has become a filming hub for [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix), and it was brought international media production companies to the state like [NBCUniversal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBCUniversal).[^cite_note-Jr_Jr_2020-73][^cite_note-Gomez_2022-74][^cite_note-Padilla_NBCUniversal_2021-75]

The [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md) was confirmed to have reached the U.S. state of New Mexico on March 11, 2020. On December 23, 2020, the New Mexico Department of Health reported 1,174 new COVID-19 cases and 40 deaths, bringing the cumulative statewide totals to 133,242 cases and 2,243 deaths since the start of the pandemic.[^cite_note-dash-76]

## Geography

Further information: [List of counties in New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_counties_in_New_Mexico)

See also: [Geography of New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_New_Mexico) and [Delaware Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware_Basin)



[Wheeler Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheeler_Peak_\(New_Mexico\)) in the [Sangre de Cristo Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangre_de_Cristo_Mountains)



[Carlsbad Caverns National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlsbad_Caverns_National_Park)



[White Sands National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Sands_National_Park)



[Rio Grande Gorge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande_Gorge) and [Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande_Gorge_Bridge)



[Shiprock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiprock)

With a total area of 121,590 square miles (314,900 km<sup>2</sup>),[^cite_note-uscensus2010_cph-2-1_area-77] New Mexico is the [fifth-largest state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_area), after Alaska, Texas, California, and Montana. Its eastern border lies along 103°W [longitude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longitude) with the state of [Oklahoma](22489.md), and 2.2 miles (3.5 kilometres) west of 103°W longitude with [Texas](29810.md) due to a 19th-century surveying error.[^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79] On the southern border, Texas makes up the eastern two-thirds, while the Mexican states of [Chihuahua](23962301.md) and [Sonora](83750.md) make up the western third, with Chihuahua making up about 90% of that. The western border with [Arizona](21883824.md) runs along the [109° 03'W](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/32nd_meridian_west_from_Washington) longitude.[^cite_note-NMSUclimate-80] The southwestern corner of the state is known as the [Bootheel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Mexico_Bootheel). The [37°N](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/37th_parallel_north) parallel forms the northern boundary with Colorado. The states of New Mexico, Colorado, Arizona, and Utah come together at the Four Corners in New Mexico's northwestern corner. Its surface water area is about 292 square miles (760 km<sup>2</sup>).[^cite_note-uscensus2010_cph-2-1_area-77]

Despite its popular depiction as mostly arid desert, New Mexico has one of the most diverse [landscapes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landscape) of any U.S. state, ranging from wide, auburn-colored deserts and [verdant grasslands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prairie), to broken [mesas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesa) and high, snow-capped peaks.[^cite_note-britannica.com-81] Close to a [third of the state is covered in timberland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_cover_by_state_and_territory_in_the_United_States), with heavily forested mountain wildernesses dominating the north. The [Sangre de Cristo Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangre_de_Cristo_Mountains), the southernmost part of the [Rocky Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Mountains), run roughly north–south along the east side of the [Rio Grande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande), in the rugged, pastoral north. The [Great Plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Plains) extend into the eastern third of the state, most notably the [Llano Estacado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Llano_Estacado) ("Staked Plain"), whose westernmost boundary is marked by the [Mescalero Ridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mescalero_Ridge) [escarpment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caprock_Escarpment). The northwestern quadrant of New Mexico is dominated by the [Colorado Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Plateau), characterized by unique volcanic formations, dry grasslands and shrublands, open [pinyon-juniper woodland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyon-juniper_woodland), and mountain forests.[^cite_note-82] The [Chihuahuan Desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chihuahuan_Desert), which is the largest in North America, extends through the south.

Over four–fifths of New Mexico is higher than 4,000 feet (1,200 meters) above sea level. The average elevation ranges from up to 8,000 feet (2,400 meters) above sea level in the northwest, to less than 4,000 feet (1,200 meters) in the southeast.[^cite_note-britannica.com-81] The highest point is [Wheeler Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheeler_Peak_\(New_Mexico\)) at over 13,160 feet (4,010 meters) in the Sangre de Cristo Mountains, while the lowest is the [Red Bluff Reservoir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bluff_Reservoir) at around 2,840 feet (870 meters), in the southeastern corner of the state.

In addition to the Rio Grande, which is tied for the [fourth-longest river in the U.S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rivers_of_the_United_States), New Mexico has four other major river systems: the [Pecos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pecos_River), [Canadian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_River), [San Juan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Juan_River_\(Colorado_River\)), and [Gila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gila_River).[^cite_note-83] Nearly bisecting New Mexico from north to south, the Rio Grande has played an influential role in the region's history; its fertile floodplain has supported human habitation since prehistoric times, and European settlers initially lived exclusively in its valleys and along its tributaries.[^cite_note-britannica.com-81] The Pecos, which flows roughly parallel to the Rio Grande to the east, was a popular route for explorers, as was the Canadian River, which rises in the mountainous north and flows east across the arid plains. The San Juan and Gila lie west of the [Continental Divide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Divide_of_the_Americas), in the northwest and southwest, respectively. Except for the Gila, all major rivers are dammed in New Mexico and provide a major water source for [irrigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irrigation) and flood control.

### Climate

New Mexico has long been known for its dry, temperate climate.[^cite_note-britannica.com-81] Overall, the state is semi-arid to arid, with areas of continental and alpine climates at higher elevations. New Mexico's statewide average precipitation is 13.7 inches (350 mm) a year, with average monthly amounts peaking in the summer, particularly in the more rugged north-central area around Albuquerque and in the south. Generally, the eastern third of the state receives the most rainfall, while the western third receives the least. Higher altitudes receive around 40 inches (1,000 mm), while the lowest elevations see as little as 8 to 10 inches (200 to 250 mm).[^cite_note-britannica.com-81]

[Köppen climate types](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification) of New Mexico, using 1991–2020 [climate normals](42778673.md)

Annual temperatures can range from 65 °F (18 °C) in the southeast to below 40 °F (4 °C) in the northern mountains,[^cite_note-NMSUclimate-80] with the average being the mid-50s °F (12 °C). During the summer, daytime temperatures can often exceed 100 °F (38 °C) at elevations below 5,000 feet (1,500 m); the average high temperature in July ranges from 99 °F (37 °C) at the lower elevations down to 78 °F (26 °C) at the higher elevations. In the colder months of November to March, many cities in New Mexico can have nighttime temperature lows in the teens above zero, or lower. The highest temperature recorded in New Mexico was 122 °F (50 °C) at the [Waste Isolation Pilot Plant (WIPP)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waste_Isolation_Pilot_Plant) near [Loving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loving,_New_Mexico) on June 27, 1994; the lowest recorded temperature is −57 °F (−49 °C) at [Ciniza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamestown,_New_Mexico) (near [Jamestown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamestown,_New_Mexico)) on January 13, 1963.[^cite_note-86]

New Mexico's stable climate and sparse population provides for clearer skies and less [light pollution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Light_pollution), making it a popular site for several major [astronomical observatories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Astronomical_observatories_in_New_Mexico), including the [Apache Point Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apache_Point_Observatory), the [Very Large Array](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Very_Large_Array), and the [Magdalena Ridge Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magdalena_Ridge_Observatory), among others.[^cite_note-87][^cite_note-88]

### Flora and fauna



[Greater roadrunner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_roadrunner) (the state bird of New Mexico)

Owing to its varied [topography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Topography), New Mexico has six distinct [vegetation zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vegetation_zone_\(latitude\)) that provide diverse sets of habitats for many plants and animals.[^cite_note-Beck-McNamee-2023a-89] The Upper Sonoran Zone is by far the most prominent, constituting about three-fourths of the state; it includes most of the plains, foothills, and valleys above 4,500 feet (1,400 meters), and is defined by prairie grasses, low piñon pines, and juniper shrubs. The [Llano Estacado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Llano_Estacado) in the east features [Shortgrass Prairie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shortgrass_Prairie) with [blue grama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bouteloua_gracilis), which sustain [bison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_bison). The [Chihuahuan Desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chihuahuan_Desert) in the south is characterized by shrubby [creosote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larrea_tridentata). The [Colorado Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Plateau) in the northwest corner of New Mexico is high desert with cold winters, featuring [sagebrush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemisia_tridentata), [shadescale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atriplex_confertifolia), [greasewood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarcobatus), and other plants adapted to the [saline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soil_salinity) and [seleniferous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selenium) soil.

The mountainous north hosts a wide array of vegetation types corresponding to elevation gradients, such as [piñon-juniper woodlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyon-juniper_woodland) near the base, through [evergreen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evergreen) [conifers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinophyta), [spruce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spruce)-[fir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fir) and [aspen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aspen) forests in the transitionary zone, and [Krummholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krummholz), and [alpine tundra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpine_tundra) at the very top.[^cite_note-Beck-McNamee-2023a-89] The [Apachian zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrean_Region) tucked into the southwestern bootheel of the state has high-calcium soil, [oak woodlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrean_pine-oak_woodlands), [Arizona cypress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cupressus_arizonica), and other plants that are not found in other parts of the state.[^cite_note-90][^cite_note-91] The southern sections of the Rio Grande and Pecos valleys have 20,000 square miles (52,000 km<sup>2</sup>) of New Mexico's best grazing land and irrigated farmland.

New Mexico's varied climate and vegetation zones consequently support diverse wildlife. [Black bears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_black_bear), [bighorn sheep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bighorn_sheep), [bobcats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobcat), [cougars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cougar), [deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deer), and [elk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elk) live in habitats above 7,000 feet (2,100 meters), while [coyotes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coyote), [jackrabbits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jackrabbit), [kangaroo rats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kangaroo_rat), [javelina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peccary), [porcupines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porcupine), [pronghorn antelope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pronghorn), [western diamondbacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crotalus_atrox), and [wild turkeys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wild_turkey) live in less mountainous and elevated regions.[^cite_note-92][^cite_note-93][^cite_note-MexicoCommission1966-94] The iconic [roadrunner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_roadrunner), which is the state bird, is abundant in the southeast. Endangered species include the [Mexican gray wolf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_wolf), which is being gradually reintroduced in the world, and [Rio Grande silvery minnow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande_silvery_minnow).[^cite_note-95] Over 500 species of birds live or migrate through New Mexico, third only to California and Mexico.[^cite_note-96]

### Public lands

New Mexico and 12 other western states together account for 93% of all federally owned land in the U.S. Roughly a third of the state, or 24.7 million of 77.8 million acres, is held by the U.S. government, the tenth-highest proportion in the country. More than half this land is under the [Bureau of Land Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Land_Management) as either [public domain land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_domain_\(land\)) or [National Conservation Lands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Conservation_Lands), while another third is managed by the [U.S. Forest Service](42652.md) as [national forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_forest_\(United_States\)).[^cite_note-97]

New Mexico was central to the early–20th-century [conservation movement](45441.md), with the [Gila Wilderness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gila_Wilderness) designated as the world's first [wilderness area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilderness_area) in 1924.[^cite_note-Mazurek-2021-98] The state also hosts nine of the country's 84 [national monuments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_monument_\(United_States\)), the most of any state after Arizona; these include the second oldest monument, [El Morro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Morro_National_Monument), created in 1906, and the [Gila Cliff Dwellings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gila_Cliff_Dwellings_National_Monument), proclaimed in 1907.[^cite_note-Mazurek-2021-98]

#### National parks in New Mexico

New Mexico's [national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._national_parks), together with [national monuments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_monument_\(United_States\)) and [trails](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Trail_System) managed by the [National Park Service](161535.md), are listed as follows:[^cite_note-99]

- [Aztec Ruins National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aztec_Ruins_National_Monument) at [Aztec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aztec,_New_Mexico)

- [Bandelier National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandelier_National_Monument) at [White Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Rock,_New_Mexico)

- [Butterfield Overland National Historic Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butterfield_Overland_National_Historic_Trail)

- [Capulin Volcano National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capulin_Volcano_National_Monument) near [Capulin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capulin,_New_Mexico)

- [Carlsbad Caverns National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlsbad_Caverns_National_Park) near [Carlsbad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlsbad,_New_Mexico)

- [Chaco Culture National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaco_Culture_National_Historical_Park) at [Nageezi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nageezi,_New_Mexico)

- [El Camino Real de Tierra Adentro National Historic Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Camino_Real_de_Tierra_Adentro_National_Historic_Trail)

- [El Malpais National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Malpais_National_Monument) near [Grants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grants,_New_Mexico)

- [El Morro National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Morro_National_Monument) in [Ramah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramah,_New_Mexico)

- [Fort Union National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Union_National_Monument) at [Watrous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watrous,_New_Mexico)

- [Gila Cliff Dwellings National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gila_Cliff_Dwellings_National_Monument) near [Silver City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silver_City,_New_Mexico)

- [Manhattan Project National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Project_National_Historical_Park) in [Los Alamos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Alamos,_New_Mexico)

- [Old Spanish National Historic Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Spanish_National_Historic_Trail)

- [Pecos National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pecos_National_Historical_Park) in [Pecos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pecos,_New_Mexico)

- [Petroglyph National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petroglyph_National_Monument) near [Albuquerque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albuquerque)

- [Salinas Pueblo Missions National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salinas_Pueblo_Missions_National_Monument) at [Mountainair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountainair,_New_Mexico)

- [Santa Fe National Historic Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe_National_Historic_Trail)

- [Valles Caldera National Preserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valles_Caldera_National_Preserve) in the [Jemez Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jemez_Mountains)

- [White Sands National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Sands_National_Park) near [Alamogordo](125944.md)

#### National conservation lands in New Mexico

New Mexico's national monuments, conservation areas, and other units of the [National Landscape Conservation System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Landscape_Conservation_System) are managed by the [Bureau of Land Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Land_Management). Units include but are not limited to:[^cite_note-100]

- [Bisti/De-Na-Zin Wilderness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bisti/De-Na-Zin_Wilderness) near [Farmington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farmington,_New_Mexico)

- [El Camino Real de Tierra Adentro National Historic Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Camino_Real_de_Tierra_Adentro_National_Historic_Trail)

- [El Malpais National Conservation Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Malpais_National_Conservation_Area) near [Grants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grants,_New_Mexico)

- [Kasha-Katuwe Tent Rocks National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kasha-Katuwe_Tent_Rocks_National_Monument) in [Cochiti Pueblo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cochiti_Pueblo)

- [Prehistoric Trackways National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prehistoric_Trackways_National_Monument) near [Las Cruces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Cruces,_New_Mexico)

- [Old Spanish National Historic Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Spanish_National_Historic_Trail)

- [Organ Mountains-Desert Peaks National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organ_Mountains-Desert_Peaks_National_Monument) near [Las Cruces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Cruces,_New_Mexico)

- [Rio Grande del Norte National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande_del_Norte_National_Monument) near [Taos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taos,_New_Mexico)

- [Rio Chama Wild and Scenic River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Chama) near [Abiquiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abiquiu)

- [Rio Grande and Red River Wild and Scenic Rivers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wild_Rivers_Recreation_Area) near [Questa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Questa,_New_Mexico)

### Environmental issues

In January 2016, New Mexico sued the [United States Environmental Protection Agency](58666.md) over negligence after the [2015 Gold King Mine waste water spill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Gold_King_Mine_waste_water_spill). The spill had caused heavy metals such as cadmium and lead, and toxins such as arsenic, to flow into the [Animas River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animas_River), polluting several states' water basins.[^cite_note-106] The state has since implemented or considered stricter regulations and harsher penalties for spills associated with resource extraction.[^cite_note-107]

New Mexico is a major producer of [greenhouse gases](21350772.md).[^cite_note-Msn-2021-108] A Colorado State University study found that the state's oil and gas industry generated 60 million metric tons of greenhouse gases in 2018, over four times more than previously estimated.[^cite_note-Msn-2021-108] The fossil fuels sector accounted for over half the state's overall emissions, which totaled 113.6 million metric tons, about 1.8% of the country's total and more than twice the national average per capita.[^cite_note-Msn-2021-108][^cite_note-Hedden-2020b-109] The New Mexico government has responded with efforts to regulate industrial emissions, promote renewable energy, and incentivize the use of electric vehicles.[^cite_note-Hedden-2020b-109][^cite_note-abqjournal.com-110]

### Settlements

See also: [List of municipalities in New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_New_Mexico), [List of census-designated places in New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_census-designated_places_in_New_Mexico), and [List of counties in New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_counties_in_New_Mexico)



New Mexico population density map

With just 17 people per square mile (6.6 people/km<sup>2</sup>), New Mexico is one of the [least densely populated states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_and_territories_of_the_United_States_by_population_density), ranking 45th out of 50; the population density of the U.S. overall is 90 people per square mile (35 people/km<sup>2</sup>). The state has 33 counties and 106 municipalities, which include cities, towns, villages, and a [consolidated city-county](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City-County), [Los Alamos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Alamos_County,_New_Mexico). Only three cities have at least 100,000 residents: Albuquerque, Rio Rancho, and Las Cruces, whose respective metropolitan areas together account for the majority of New Mexico's population.[^cite_note-111]

Residents are concentrated in the north-central region, anchored by the state's largest city, Albuquerque. Centered in [Bernalillo County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernalillo_County,_New_Mexico), the [Albuquerque metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albuquerque_metropolitan_area) includes New Mexico's third-largest city, [Rio Rancho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Rancho,_New_Mexico), and has a population of over 918,000, accounting for one-third of all New Mexicans. It is adjacent to [Santa Fe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe,_New_Mexico), the capital and fourth-largest city. Altogether, the [Albuquerque–Santa Fe–Los Alamos combined statistical area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albuquerque%E2%80%93Santa_Fe%E2%80%93Los_Alamos_combined_statistical_area) includes more than 1.17 million people, nearly 60% of the state population.

New Mexico's other major center of population is in the south-central area around [Las Cruces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Cruces,_New_Mexico), its second-largest city and the largest city in the state's southern region. The Las Cruces metropolitan area has roughly 214,000 residents, and with neighboring [El Paso, Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Paso,_Texas), forms a [combined statistical area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Paso%E2%80%93Las_Cruces,_Texas%E2%80%93New_Mexico,_combined_statistical_area) numbering over 1 million.[^cite_note-112]

New Mexico hosts 23 federally recognized tribal reservations, including part of the Navajo Nation, the largest and most populous tribe; of these, 11 hold [off-reservation trust lands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Off-reservation_trust_land) elsewhere in the state. The vast majority of federally recognized tribes are concentrated in the northwest, followed by the north-central region.

Like several other southwestern states, New Mexico has numerous [*colonias*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonia_\(United_States\))—[unincorporated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unincorporated_area), low-income [slums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slum) characterized by abject poverty, absence of basic services (such as water and sewage), and scarce housing and infrastructure.[^cite_note-113] The University of New Mexico estimates there are 118 colonias in the state, though the [U.S. Department of Housing and Urban Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Housing_and_Urban_Development) identifies roughly 150.[^cite_note-114] Most are along the [Mexico–United States border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico%E2%80%93United_States_border).

New Mexico [population pyramid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_pyramid)

The [2020 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census) recorded a population of 2,117,522, an increase of 2.8% from 2,059,179 in the [2010 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_census).[^cite_note-PopEstUS-118] This was the lowest rate of growth in the western U.S. after Wyoming, and among the slowest nationwide.[^cite_note-Bryan-2021-119] By comparison, between [2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_United_States_census) and 2010, New Mexico's population had increased by 11.7% from 1,819,046—a faster rate than the national average.[^cite_note-bber.unm.edu-120] A report commissioned in 2021 by the New Mexico Legislature attributed the state's slow growth to a [negative net migration rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_migration_rate), particularly among those 18 or younger, and to a 19% decline in the birth rate.[^cite_note-Bryan-2021-119] However, growth among Hispanics and Native Americans remained healthy.[^cite_note-Bryan-2021-119][^cite_note-121]

The U.S. Census Bureau estimated a slight decrease in population, with 3,333 fewer people from July 2021 to July 2022.[^cite_note-Narvaiz-2022-122] This was attributed to deaths exceeding births by roughly 5,000, with [net migration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_migration_rate) mitigating the loss by 1,389.[^cite_note-Narvaiz-2022-122]

More than half of New Mexicans (51.4%) were born in the state; 37.9% were born in another state; 1.1% were born in either Puerto Rico, an [island territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territories_of_the_United_States), or abroad to at least one American parent; and 9.4% were foreign born (compared to a national average of roughly 12%).[^cite_note-123] Almost a quarter of the population (22.7%) was under the age of 18, and the state's median age of 38.4 is [slightly above the national average](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_median_age) of 38.2. New Mexico's somewhat older population is partly reflective of its popularity among retirees: It ranked as the most popular retirement destination in 2018,[^cite_note-124] with an estimated 42% of new residents being retired.[^cite_note-125]

Hispanics and Latinos constitute nearly half of all residents (49.3%), giving New Mexico the highest proportion of Hispanic ancestry among the fifty states. This broad classification includes [descendants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_American) of Spanish [colonists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonist) who settled between the 16th and 18th centuries, as well as recent immigrants from Latin America (particularly Mexico and Central America).

From 2000 to 2010, the number of persons in poverty increased to 400,779, or approximately one-fifth of the population.[^cite_note-bber.unm.edu-120] The 2020 census recorded a slightly reduced poverty rate of 18.2%, albeit the [third highest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_poverty_rate) among U.S. states, compared to a national average of 10.5%. Poverty disproportionately affects minorities, with about one-third of African Americans and Native Americans living in poverty, compared with less than a fifth of whites and roughly a tenth of Asians; likewise, New Mexico ranks 49th among states for education equality by race and 32nd for its racial gap in income.[^cite_note-usnews.com-126]

New Mexico's population is among the most difficult to count, according to the Center for Urban Research at the [City University of New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_University_of_New_York), due to the state's size, sparse population, and numerous isolated communities.[^cite_note-Bryan-2021-119] Likewise, the Census Bureau estimated that roughly 43% of the state's population (about 900,000 people) live in such "hard-to-count" areas.[^cite_note-Bryan-2021-119] In response, the New Mexico government invested heavily in public outreach to increase census participation, resulting in a final tally that exceeded earlier estimates and outperformed several neighboring states.[^cite_note-127]

According to [HUD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Housing_and_Urban_Development)'s 2022 [Annual Homeless Assessment Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annual_Homeless_Assessment_Report_to_Congress), there were an estimated 2,560 [homeless](19394651.md) people in New Mexico.[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129]

### Race and ethnicity





Largest alone or in any combination ethnic origin by county in New Mexico, per the 2020 census



Largest Non-Hispanic Native American ancestry by county and numbers of people reporting "Native American Alone"

New Mexico is one of seven ["majority-minority"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majority_minority_in_the_United_States) states where [non-Hispanic whites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Hispanic_whites) constitute less than half the population.[^cite_note-130] As early as 1940, roughly half the population was estimated to be nonwhite.[^cite_note-131] Before becoming a state in 1912, New Mexico was among the few U.S. territories that were predominantly nonwhite, which contributed to its delayed admission into the Union.[^cite_note-132]

The largest ethnic group is [Hispanic and Latino Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic_and_Latino_Americans); according to the [2020 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census) they account for nearly half the state's population, at 47.7%; they include [Hispanos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanos_of_New_Mexico) descended from pre-United States settlers and more recent successions of [Mexican Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Americans).[^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134][^cite_note-NBC_News_2021-135]

Some 200,000 residents, about one-tenth of the population, are of [Indigenous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas) descent,[^cite_note-Observer-2022-136] ranking third in size,[^cite_note-137] and second proportionally,[^cite_note-138] among all states nationwide. There are 23 federally recognized tribal nations, each with its distinct culture, history, and identity. Both the [Navajo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navajo_Nation) and [Apache](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apache_Tribe) share [Athabaskan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athabaskan) origin, with the latter living on three federal [reservations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_American_reservation) in the state.[^cite_note-139] The [Navajo Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navajo_Nation), which spans over 16 million acres (6.5 million [ha](24919513.md)), mostly in neighboring [Arizona](21883824.md), is the largest reservation in the U.S., with one-third of its members living in New Mexico.[^cite_note-Observer-2022-136] [Pueblo Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo_Indians), who share a similar lifestyle but are culturally and linguistically distinct, live in 19 [pueblos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puebloans) scattered throughout the state, which collectively span over 2 million acres (800,000 ha).[^cite_note-140] Many indigenous New Mexicans have moved to urban areas throughout the state, and some cities such as Gallup are major hubs of Native American culture.[^cite_note-Beck-McNamee-2023b-141] New Mexico is also a hub for indigenous communities beyond its borders: the annual [Gathering of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gathering_of_Nations), which began in 1983, has been described as the largest *[pow wow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pow_wow)* in the U.S., drawing hundreds of native tribes from across North America.[^cite_note-142]

Almost half of New Mexicans claim Hispanic origin; many are descendants of colonial settlers called [*Hispanos* or *Nuevomexicanos*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanos_of_New_Mexico), who settled mostly in the north of the state between the 16th and 18th centuries; by contrast, the majority of Mexican immigrants reside in the south. Some Hispanos claim [Jewish ancestry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Who_is_a_Jew%3F#New_Mexico's_Crypto-Jews) through descendance from *[conversos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Converso)* or [Crypto-Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crypto-Judaism) among early Spanish colonists.[^cite_note-143] Many New Mexicans speak a unique dialect known as [New Mexican Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Mexican_Spanish), which was shaped by the region's historical isolation and various cultural influences; New Mexican Spanish lacks certain vocabulary from other Spanish dialects and uses numerous Native American words for local features, as well as anglicized words that express American concepts and modern inventions.[^cite_note-144]

New Mexico has the fourth-largest Native American community in the U.S., at over 200,000; comprising roughly one-tenth of all residents, this is the second-largest population by percentage after Alaska.[^cite_note-QuickFacts-145][^cite_note-demo-146] New Mexico is also the only state besides Alaska where indigenous people have maintained a stable proportion of the population for over a century: In 1890, Native Americans made up 9.4% of New Mexico's population, almost the same percentage as in 2020.[^cite_note-Gibson-Jung-2005-147] By contrast, during that same period, neighboring Arizona went from one-third indigenous to less than 5%.[^cite_note-Gibson-Jung-2005-147]

New Mexico's population consists of many [mestizo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mestizo) Indo-Hispano groups, including Hispanos of [Oasisamerican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oasisamerica) descent and [Indigenous Mexican American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_Mexican_Americans) with [Mesoamerican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesoamerica) ancestry.[^cite_note-Wheelwright_2012-148][^cite_note-Sanchez_2023-149]
